I really am trying to keep a lid on it, I promise, it just... it slips out before I can stop it. I really *want* to feel happy about it. But it’s like there’s just nothing in me that can respond right now. I just feel empty. \n\nI'm so sorry, I know that sounds awful of me as a father and a husband, I really do love them. It's just hard to feel it right now. Is... is that what you mean by depressed?\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling anxious most of the time lately?\n\nPatient: Um... anxious? I don't... I don't think so. I hope it's alright that I'm saying no to that, I really want to give you the right answers so you can help me. But I don't really feel nervous or worried, you know? Not like a panic or anything. \n\nIt's really just that... that shortness of temper and that empty feeling I was talking about. I'm not wasting your time with all this, am I? I just... I haven't really noticed being anxious on top of the rest of it. Is it okay if I don't have that one?\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way?\n\nPatient: Um, well, it's been... I guess about three weeks now. Ever since, um... well, there was an incident at work. Um, sleep... yeah, sleep has been a little tricky lately. I mean, I'm really trying to get my eight hours, I know how important rest is for recovery and all that. I don't want you to think I'm just staying up late on purpose or anything.

Um, avoiding things? Well... I mean, I try not to. I really try to just go about my normal day, because I know I need to move past it. I don't want to be one of those people who just hides from the world, you know?

Um, detached? Like... outside my body? No, I don't... I don't think I've felt anything like that. I mean, I'm definitely still here, in my own skin, you know?

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Um, guilty? Oh, no, no. I mean, it was an accident with the machinery, you know? Nobody could have predicted it. I know I was the manager on duty, and... well, I guess part of me wishes I had caught the pressure gauge issue sooner, but I'm really trying not to beat myself up over it. 

I know accidents happen, and I did everything by the book once the alarm went off. I made sure everyone evacuated. I don't want you to think I'm sitting around blaming myself all day, because I know that's not productive for my mental health. I'm really trying to focus on moving forward.

Um, activities I used to enjoy? Well... I mean, I'm still trying to keep up with things. I don't want to just sit around and mope, you know? I know how important it is to stay active.

I guess... well, usually I'd be out in the garage on the weekends, working on my old Chevy. It's kind of my pride and joy. But lately, I just haven't really felt like going out there. I think I'm just a bit drained from everything, so I've been resting inside instead. It's not that I don't care about it anymore, I just... I think I need a little break right now. 

And I usually bowl in a league on Thursday nights, but I skipped the last couple of weeks. I told the guys I was just fighting off a bug. I really am planning to go back soon, though. I promise I'm not giving up on my hobbies or anything. Is it normal to just need a little downtime after something stressful? I just want to make sure I'm not doing the wrong thing by taking a breather.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Um, losing interest completely? Well, like I said before about the car and the bowling... I haven't totally lost interest, I just need a break. I'm really trying to keep things normal at home, too. I don't want to be a burden on my family, you know?

I guess... well, it's just been a little hard to feel, um, happy about things right now. Like, my wife will make a nice dinner, or my kids will come in talking all excited about soccer practice, just like they always do. And I know I should feel glad to be there with them, sitting at the table. I *want* to feel glad. But lately, it's like... it's like there's just nothing in me that can respond to it. I just feel kind of blank inside. 

But I'm sure that's just because I'm so tired from everything, right? I'm not turning into a bad husband or father or anything. I really do love them, I just... I think my emotions are just a little worn out right now. That makes sense, doesn't it? I just want to make sure I'm not doing any permanent damage to my family by being a little detached right now.

Is... is it okay that I'm coming to you for something like this?\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling irritable most of the time lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah, exactly... like I was just saying, it feels like it's almost all the time right now. I just keep, um, blowing up over the absolute smallest things. Like those operations calls I mentioned... someone will ask a standard question and I just snap at them. And then I do it with my family, too. \n\nIt's just so out of character for me, because that is really not how I’ve ever handled things, and I feel incredibly guilty about it. I really am trying to keep a lid on it, I promise, it just... it slips out before I can stop it. I hope I'm not sounding like a terrible person, it's just... does that answer your question?\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling anxious or depressed most of the time lately?\n\nPatient: Well... I don't know if I'd use the word depressed, exactly. I really don't want to sound ungrateful, because I have a good life. But... maybe? I guess I just feel sort of... blank? \n\nI feel terrible even saying this out loud, but... like, my wife will make dinner, or my kids will be talking about soccer like normal, and... and I know I should feel glad to be there with them. I really *want* to feel happy about it. But it’s like there’s just nothing in me that can respond right now. I just feel empty. \n\nI'm so sorry, I know that sounds awful of me as a father and a husband, I really do love them. It's just hard to feel it right now. Is... is that what you mean by depressed?\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling anxious most of the time lately?\n\nPatient: Um... anxious? I don't... I don't think so. I hope it's alright that I'm saying no to that, I really want to give you the right answers so you can help me. But I don't really feel nervous or worried, you know? Not like a panic or anything. \n\nIt's really just that... that shortness of temper and that empty feeling I was talking about. I'm not wasting your time with all this, am I? I just... I haven't really noticed being anxious on top of the rest of it. Is it okay if I don't have that one?\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way?\n\nPatient: Um, well, it's been... I guess about three weeks now. Ever since, um... well, there was an incident at work. A valve failure at the facility, and... and someone got hurt. \n\nI really hope I'm not making too big a deal out of it, I know people deal with stressful things at their jobs all the time and handle it way better than I am right now. I just... ever since that happened, that's when all this started. Does that make sense? Is that the kind of timeline you were looking for?\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself enjoying things you normally like to do, or has that changed recently?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I guess that kind of goes back to what I was saying about feeling blank. I still try to do the things I normally like, you know? Like, I'll go out to the garage to work on my woodworking projects, or I'll sit down to watch a game... \n\nI really try to enjoy it, I promise I do. I know how important it is to keep up with hobbies and stay active. But... I don't know, it just feels like going through the motions lately. I'll be out there sanding a piece of wood for an hour and realize I haven't really felt anything about it at all. \n\nI'm sorry, I know I should be trying harder to snap out of it and just enjoy my life. I really hope you don't think I'm just being lazy or feeling sorry for myself. Is it normal to lose interest like that?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight recently?\n\nPatient: Um, weight changes? No, I don't... I don't think so. I haven't really been keeping track, to be honest. I mean, my clothes still fit the same, so I think I'm doing okay there. \n\nI hope it's alright that I haven't been weighing myself. I know I should probably pay closer attention to my health, especially right now. But I haven't noticed anything drastic. I'm still trying to eat my regular meals and everything, I promise. Is that... is it okay if my weight hasn't changed? I'm giving you the right information, right?\n\nDoctor: Have you been having any problems with your sleep lately?\n\nPatient: Um, sleep... yeah, sleep has been a little tricky lately. I mean, I'm really trying to get my eight hours, I know how important rest is for recovery and all that. I don't want you to think I'm just staying up late on purpose or anything. \n\nBut... well, most nights I just lie there, and I keep hearing that alarm from the facility in my head. And then, when I finally do manage to doze off, I... I wake up sweating. I keep having these dreams about the smoke, and people yelling, and... \n\nI'm sorry, I know everyone has bad dreams sometimes. I really try to just go back to sleep, but by morning I feel like I haven't slept at all. I hope that's not too unusual? I'm really trying my best to get back into a normal routine, I promise. Is it okay that I'm struggling with this right now?\n\nDoctor: Have you been avoiding people, places, or things that remind you of the incident at work?\n\nPatient: Um, avoiding things? Well... I mean, I try not to. I really try to just go about my normal day, because I know I need to move past it. I don't want to be one of those people who just hides from the world, you know? \n\nI guess... well, maybe I have been taking a different route to the grocery store so I don't drive past the industrial park. And I haven't really been answering calls from the guys on my shift. I know I should, I know they're probably just checking on me, and I feel terrible for ignoring them. I just... I don't want to bring them down or make a big deal out of things. \n\nI'm still going to the places I need to go, mostly. I promise I'm not just shutting myself in. Is it... is it bad that I'm taking a little space from work stuff right now? I hope you don't think I'm just running away from my responsibilities.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're detached from yourself or your surroundings, like you're observing things from outside your body?\n\nPatient: Um, detached? Like... outside my body? No, I don't... I don't think I've felt anything like that. I mean, I'm definitely still here, in my own skin, you know? \n\nI guess sometimes my mind wanders a bit, or I get a little distracted, but I think that's just because I'm tired from not sleeping great. But I'm definitely not, like, floating above myself or anything weird like that. I'm staying grounded. I've been making sure to focus on what I'm doing, like when I'm making breakfast or doing stuff around the house. \n\nI hope that's the right answer? I just want to make sure I'm giving you an accurate picture. I don't want to sound like I'm losing my grip on reality or anything, because I promise I'm holding it together. Is it normal that I don't feel that way?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel guilty about what happened at the facility?\n\nPatient: Um, guilty? Oh, no, no. I mean, it was an accident with the machinery, you know? Nobody could have predicted it. I know I was the manager on duty, and... well, I guess part of me wishes I had caught the pressure gauge issue sooner, but I'm really trying not to beat myself up over it. \n\nI know accidents happen, and I did everything by the book once the alarm went off. I made sure everyone evacuated. I don't want you to think I'm sitting around blaming myself all day, because I know that's not productive for my mental health. I'm really trying to focus on moving forward. \n\nI mean, it's natural to have a few \"what ifs,\" right? But I'm definitely not consumed by guilt or anything. I hope it's okay that I feel that way? I just want to make sure I'm handling all this the right way.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you used to enjoy, but now find you have no interest in doing at all?\n\nPatient: Um, activities I used to enjoy? Well... I mean, I'm still trying to keep up with things. I don't want to just sit around and mope, you know? I know how important it is to stay active.\n\nI guess... well, usually I'd be out in the garage on the weekends, working on my old Chevy. It's kind of my pride and joy. But lately, I just haven't really felt like going out there. I think I'm just a bit drained from everything, so I've been resting inside instead. It's not that I don't care about it anymore, I just... I think I need a little break right now. \n\nAnd I usually bowl in a league on Thursday nights, but I skipped the last couple of weeks. I told the guys I was just fighting off a bug. I really am planning to go back soon, though. I promise I'm not giving up on my hobbies or anything. Is it normal to just need a little downtime after something stressful? I just want to make sure I'm not doing the wrong thing by taking a breather.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself thinking that you could have done something to prevent the accident at the facility?\n\nPatient: Um, thinking I could have prevented it? No, no, I wouldn't say I sit around thinking that. I mean, I told you, right? I did everything by the book when the alarm went off. \n\nI guess... well, sure, sometimes a thought pops up, like, maybe if I had checked the pressure gauge five minutes earlier... but I push that right out of my head. I know it's not helpful to dwell on it. I really try to just focus on the fact that I got everyone out safe. That's the important part, right? \n\nI don't want you to think I'm obsessing over it or going crazy with \"what ifs.\" I'm really trying to keep a level head about the whole thing. I'm handling it okay, aren't I? I mean, a few passing thoughts are pretty normal, right?\n\nDoctor: Are there any specific memories or reminders of the incident at work that you've been trying to avoid thinking about?\n\nPatient: Um, avoiding things? No, not really. I mean, I'm not hiding under the covers or anything like that. I know I have to face reality. \n\nI guess... well, I haven't driven past the industrial park since it happened. But that's just because I've been taking the highway instead. It's a little faster anyway, you know? And, um, I haven't really looked at the incident report the company sent over. I just figured I'd get to it when I'm feeling a little more rested. \n\nBut I'm not, like, actively running away from it. I know I need to deal with it eventually, and I will. I'm just pacing myself, I guess. That's a healthy way to handle it, right? I just don't want to overwhelm myself and take a step backward. I'm doing the right thing by taking it slow, aren't I?\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you have completely lost interest in since the incident at work?\n\nPatient: Um, losing interest completely? Well, like I said before about the car and the bowling... I haven't totally lost interest, I just need a break. I'm really trying to keep things normal at home, too. I don't want to be a burden on my family, you know?\n\nI guess... well, it's just been a little hard to feel, um, happy about things right now. Like, my wife will make a nice dinner, or my kids will come in talking all excited about soccer practice, just like they always do. And I know I should feel glad to be there with them, sitting at the table. I *want* to feel glad. But lately, it's like... it's like there's just nothing in me that can respond to it. I just feel kind of blank inside. \n\nBut I'm sure that's just because I'm so tired from everything, right? I'm not turning into a bad husband or father or anything. I really do love them, I just... I think my emotions are just a little worn out right now. That makes sense, doesn't it? I just want to make sure I'm not doing any permanent damage to my family by being a little detached right now.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you have completely stopped doing since the incident at work?\n\nPatient: Um, completely stopped? No, no, I wouldn't say that at all. I mean, like I mentioned before with the bowling and working on the car, it's really just a pause, you know? I haven't quit anything forever. \n\nI'm still doing what I need to do. I'm keeping up with my chores around the house, taking care of the yard, helping the kids with their things... I wouldn't just drop my responsibilities. People rely on me, and I take that seriously. \n\nI know it's really important to keep a routine going after something like this happens, right? I read that somewhere, that you're supposed to just keep moving forward to get back to normal. So I'm really trying my best to just keep putting one foot in front of the other and not let things slide. That's exactly what I should be doing, don't you think? I just want to make sure I'm handling all this the right way.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost interest in activities that you used to find pleasurable?\n\nPatient: Um, I think that's kind of similar to what we were just talking about, right? With the bowling league and working on the car... I wouldn't say I've lost interest in the things I enjoy. Not at all. \n\nI mean, I still know they're fun. I'm just... taking a little breather from them right now. I still try to do nice things, especially with my wife and kids. Like, we watched a movie the other night, and I made sure to sit right there with them on the couch. I might not have been laughing out loud as much as I usually do, but I'm still participating. I haven't just thrown in the towel on having a good time. \n\nIt just takes a little more energy right now to get into the swing of things, you know? But I'm still making the effort. That's the best way to handle it, right? Just keep trying to enjoy the normal stuff until it feels completely natural again? I really don't want you to think I'm just giving up on the things I like to do. I'm doing okay with it all, aren't I?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"

You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Um, flashbacks? That sounds a little... intense. I don't know if I'd use that word exactly. It's not like in the movies or anything. 

But, well, I guess sometimes... I can just be sitting at the kitchen table, you know, having coffee, or trying to catch up on some work emails from home, and... suddenly it's like I'm right back there. I see the flash from that valve blowing, and the smoke filling up the aisle. It just kind of... shoves everything else out of my head for a second. 

It's just a quick thing, though! I shake it off pretty fast. I mean, it makes sense that it pops into my head sometimes, right? It was a pretty big deal at the facility. It's perfectly normal to replay it a little bit, isn't it? I'm not letting it completely take over my day or anything, I promise. I just get back to whatever I was doing. That's the best way to handle it, right?

I don't want to be a burden on my family, you know?\n\nI guess... well, it's just been a little hard to feel, um, happy about things right now. Like, my wife will make a nice dinner, or my kids will come in talking all excited about soccer practice, just like they always do. So I'm really trying my best to just keep putting one foot in front of the other and not let things slide. That's exactly what I should be doing, don't you think?
